People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

I don’t know if it’s, like, a “proper” order or anything, but I do end up going back over the same things—outlets, locks, tools, even dust—and checking or fixing them again and again until it feels… not exactly okay, but closer.  \n\nI try to tell myself I’m just being careful, but it can take most of the day, and even when I’m worn out I still don’t feel sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checks and fixes are necessary to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s what it feels like. I know it might sound a bit over the top, and I don’t want to make it seem like I’m not reasonable, but if I don’t go back over the outlets, the locks, the tools, even the dust and little things around the house, I get this feeling like something could go wrong because I didn’t do it right.\n\nSo I keep checking and fixing, sometimes most of the day, until I’m worn out. And even then, I don’t really feel completely sure enough to leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking and fixing these things helps reduce your anxiety or worry about something bad happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… for a little while, I guess it does. Like if I go back over the outlets or the locks or the tools, or clean up the dust the “right” way, I feel a bit less worried for a moment.\n\nBut then it doesn’t really last. I end up checking and fixing the same things again and again, and it can take most of the day. By the end I’m worn out, and I still don’t feel completely sure enough to just leave it alone. I know that probably sounds like a lot… but that’s kind of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to prevent something specific from happening when you check and fix things, or is it more about feeling unsure and trying to feel more certain?\n\nPatient: I think it’s… both, maybe. I mean, there are specific things that pop into my head, and I don’t really like saying them because they sound awful, but… even when I know I did something right, I’ll suddenly think, what if I missed a loose wire and the house burns down? Or what if I left a nail somewhere and my wife gets hurt?\n\nI try to tell myself that’s not realistic, or push it away, but then it comes back even stronger. So I check and fix things to make that feeling go down and to feel more certain, I guess. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it… that’s just what happens.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um… not so much symmetrical, I don’t think. It’s more that it has to feel done enough, or done the right way, if that makes sense.

I don’t know if it’s, like, a “proper” order or anything, but I do end up going back over the same things—outlets, locks, tools, even dust—and checking or fixing them again and again until it feels… not exactly okay, but closer.  \n\nI try to tell myself I’m just being careful, but it can take most of the day, and even when I’m worn out I still don’t feel sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checks and fixes are necessary to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s what it feels like. I know it might sound a bit over the top, and I don’t want to make it seem like I’m not reasonable, but if I don’t go back over the outlets, the locks, the tools, even the dust and little things around the house, I get this feeling like something could go wrong because I didn’t do it right.\n\nSo I keep checking and fixing, sometimes most of the day, until I’m worn out. And even then, I don’t really feel completely sure enough to leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking and fixing these things helps reduce your anxiety or worry about something bad happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… for a little while, I guess it does. Like if I go back over the outlets or the locks or the tools, or clean up the dust the “right” way, I feel a bit less worried for a moment.\n\nBut then it doesn’t really last. I end up checking and fixing the same things again and again, and it can take most of the day. By the end I’m worn out, and I still don’t feel completely sure enough to just leave it alone. I know that probably sounds like a lot… but that’s kind of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to prevent something specific from happening when you check and fix things, or is it more about feeling unsure and trying to feel more certain?\n\nPatient: I think it’s… both, maybe. I mean, there are specific things that pop into my head, and I don’t really like saying them because they sound awful, but… even when I know I did something right, I’ll suddenly think, what if I missed a loose wire and the house burns down? Or what if I left a nail somewhere and my wife gets hurt?\n\nI try to tell myself that’s not realistic, or push it away, but then it comes back even stronger. So I check and fix things to make that feeling go down and to feel more certain, I guess. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it… that’s just what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that looks or feels 'just right' or symmetrical?\n\nPatient: Um… not so much symmetrical, I don’t think. It’s more that it has to feel done enough, or done the right way, if that makes sense.\n\nLike with the outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house, I’ll keep going back over them and fixing little things again and again. I know it probably sounds excessive, but I can spend most of the day doing that, and even when I’m completely worn out, I still don’t feel quite sure enough to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a specific or perfect way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not perfect like I’m trying to make it look nice, exactly, but it has to feel like I did it the right way.\n\nI’ll go back over the same outlets, locks, tools, or dust in the house and check or fix them again and again. I don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but it can take up most of my day sometimes. And even when I’m worn out from it, I still don’t feel totally sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be arranged in a specific pattern or order?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a pattern or order, really. At least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nIt’s more like I’m trying to make sure it’s okay, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t have to arrange things in a certain sequence or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be a certain way or in a certain place to feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it does, but not really like everything has to be in some exact spot or lined up.\n\nIt’s more that outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house have to feel like they’re okay before I can leave them. I’ll check or fix the same things over and over, and I know that probably sounds like a lot… but I can lose most of the day to it. Even after I’m exhausted, it still doesn’t feel settled enough. Does that answer what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I end up checking and fixing the same things again and again, and it can take most of the day. By the end I’m worn out, and I still don’t feel completely sure enough to just leave it alone. I know it probably sounds excessive, but I can spend most of the day doing that, and even when I’m completely worn out, I still don’t feel quite sure enough to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a specific or perfect way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be arranged in a specific pattern or order?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a pattern or order, really. At least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nIt’s more like I’m trying to make sure it’s okay, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t have to arrange things in a certain sequence or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be a certain way or in a certain place to feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it does, but not really like everything has to be in some exact spot or lined up.\n\nIt’s more that outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house have to feel like they’re okay before I can leave them. I’ll check or fix the same things over and over, and I know that probably sounds like a lot… but I can lose most of the day to it. Even after I’m exhausted, it still doesn’t feel settled enough. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be symmetrical or arranged in a precise way to feel right?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think it’s symmetry exactly. I’m not trying to make things match or line them up perfectly, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I need it to feel okay before I can move on. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really a certain number, no… I don’t count it out like that.

It’s more that I go back over the same things — the outlets, locks, tools, dust — and I’ll check or fix them again and again until it feels safe enough or okay enough. But even then, honestly, it still doesn’t always feel settled. I can end up worn out from it and still not feel sure I can leave it alone.

I hope that makes sense… it’s not like “three times” or “five times,” more just until I feel like I can stop, if I can.

I’m not trying to be dramatic about it… that’s just what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that looks or feels 'just right' or symmetrical?\n\nPatient: Um… not so much symmetrical, I don’t think. It’s more that it has to feel done enough, or done the right way, if that makes sense.\n\nLike with the outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house, I’ll keep going back over them and fixing little things again and again. I know it probably sounds excessive, but I can spend most of the day doing that, and even when I’m completely worn out, I still don’t feel quite sure enough to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a specific or perfect way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not perfect like I’m trying to make it look nice, exactly, but it has to feel like I did it the right way.\n\nI’ll go back over the same outlets, locks, tools, or dust in the house and check or fix them again and again. I don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but it can take up most of my day sometimes. And even when I’m worn out from it, I still don’t feel totally sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be arranged in a specific pattern or order?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a pattern or order, really. At least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nIt’s more like I’m trying to make sure it’s okay, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t have to arrange things in a certain sequence or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be a certain way or in a certain place to feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it does, but not really like everything has to be in some exact spot or lined up.\n\nIt’s more that outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house have to feel like they’re okay before I can leave them. I’ll check or fix the same things over and over, and I know that probably sounds like a lot… but I can lose most of the day to it. Even after I’m exhausted, it still doesn’t feel settled enough. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be symmetrical or arranged in a precise way to feel right?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think it’s symmetry exactly. I’m not trying to make things match or line them up perfectly, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I need it to feel okay before I can move on. But not in a precise arranged way, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be done a certain number of times to feel right?\n\nPatient: Not really a certain number, no… I don’t count it out like that.\n\nIt’s more that I go back over the same things — the outlets, locks, tools, dust — and I’ll check or fix them again and again until it feels safe enough or okay enough. But even then, honestly, it still doesn’t always feel settled. I can end up worn out from it and still not feel sure I can leave it alone.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it’s not like “three times” or “five times,” more just until I feel like I can stop, if I can.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Perfect or flawless… I don’t know if I’d call it that, exactly. I’m not trying to make the house look perfect.

I don’t know if it’s, like, a “proper” order or anything, but I do end up going back over the same things—outlets, locks, tools, even dust—and checking or fixing them again and again until it feels… not exactly okay, but closer.  \n\nI try to tell myself I’m just being careful, but it can take most of the day, and even when I’m worn out I still don’t feel sure enough to just leave it alone. I’m not trying to make things match or line them up perfectly, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I need it to feel okay before I can move on. But not in a precise arranged way, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be done a certain number of times to feel right?\n\nPatient: Not really a certain number, no… I don’t count it out like that.\n\nIt’s more that I go back over the same things — the outlets, locks, tools, dust — and I’ll check or fix them again and again until it feels safe enough or okay enough. But even then, honestly, it still doesn’t always feel settled. I can end up worn out from it and still not feel sure I can leave it alone.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it’s not like “three times” or “five times,” more just until I feel like I can stop, if I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be perfect or flawless in any way?\n\nPatient: Perfect or flawless… I don’t know if I’d call it that, exactly. I’m not trying to make the house look perfect.\n\nIt’s more like I can’t leave things alone unless they feel okay enough. So I keep going back to the outlets, locks, tools, or dust, checking and fixing them again and again. I know it sounds like a lot, and I’m not proud of it, but it can take up most of the day. Even when I’m worn out, I still don’t always feel sure enough to stop.\n\nIs that what you mean by perfect?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.     "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42.0, F42.1, F42.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a hard time leaving things alone at home. I keep going back to check outlets, locks, tools, and even dust, and then I try to fix or straighten things again and again. It takes up most of my day, honestly, and by the end I’m worn out… but I still don’t really feel sure enough to stop. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts or images that keep repeating in your mind and are disturbing or upsetting to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but yes. Even when I’m pretty sure I did something right, I’ll get these thoughts that maybe I missed a loose wire and the house could burn down, or that I left a nail somewhere and my wife could get hurt. I know it probably sounds excessive… I try to push it out of my head, but then it comes back even stronger.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks or fixes in a specific way or order to feel okay?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know if it’s, like, a “proper” order or anything, but I do end up going back over the same things—outlets, locks, tools, even dust—and checking or fixing them again and again until it feels… not exactly okay, but closer.  \n\nI try to tell myself I’m just being careful, but it can take most of the day, and even when I’m worn out I still don’t feel sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checks and fixes are necessary to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s what it feels like. I know it might sound a bit over the top, and I don’t want to make it seem like I’m not reasonable, but if I don’t go back over the outlets, the locks, the tools, even the dust and little things around the house, I get this feeling like something could go wrong because I didn’t do it right.\n\nSo I keep checking and fixing, sometimes most of the day, until I’m worn out. And even then, I don’t really feel completely sure enough to leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking and fixing these things helps reduce your anxiety or worry about something bad happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… for a little while, I guess it does. Like if I go back over the outlets or the locks or the tools, or clean up the dust the “right” way, I feel a bit less worried for a moment.\n\nBut then it doesn’t really last. I end up checking and fixing the same things again and again, and it can take most of the day. By the end I’m worn out, and I still don’t feel completely sure enough to just leave it alone. I know that probably sounds like a lot… but that’s kind of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to prevent something specific from happening when you check and fix things, or is it more about feeling unsure and trying to feel more certain?\n\nPatient: I think it’s… both, maybe. I mean, there are specific things that pop into my head, and I don’t really like saying them because they sound awful, but… even when I know I did something right, I’ll suddenly think, what if I missed a loose wire and the house burns down? Or what if I left a nail somewhere and my wife gets hurt?\n\nI try to tell myself that’s not realistic, or push it away, but then it comes back even stronger. So I check and fix things to make that feeling go down and to feel more certain, I guess. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it… that’s just what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that looks or feels 'just right' or symmetrical?\n\nPatient: Um… not so much symmetrical, I don’t think. It’s more that it has to feel done enough, or done the right way, if that makes sense.\n\nLike with the outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house, I’ll keep going back over them and fixing little things again and again. I know it probably sounds excessive, but I can spend most of the day doing that, and even when I’m completely worn out, I still don’t feel quite sure enough to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a specific or perfect way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not perfect like I’m trying to make it look nice, exactly, but it has to feel like I did it the right way.\n\nI’ll go back over the same outlets, locks, tools, or dust in the house and check or fix them again and again. I don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but it can take up most of my day sometimes. And even when I’m worn out from it, I still don’t feel totally sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be arranged in a specific pattern or order?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a pattern or order, really. At least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nIt’s more like I’m trying to make sure it’s okay, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t have to arrange things in a certain sequence or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be a certain way or in a certain place to feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it does, but not really like everything has to be in some exact spot or lined up.\n\nIt’s more that outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house have to feel like they’re okay before I can leave them. I’ll check or fix the same things over and over, and I know that probably sounds like a lot… but I can lose most of the day to it. Even after I’m exhausted, it still doesn’t feel settled enough. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be symmetrical or arranged in a precise way to feel right?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think it’s symmetry exactly. I’m not trying to make things match or line them up perfectly, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I need it to feel okay before I can move on. But not in a precise arranged way, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be done a certain number of times to feel right?\n\nPatient: Not really a certain number, no… I don’t count it out like that.\n\nIt’s more that I go back over the same things — the outlets, locks, tools, dust — and I’ll check or fix them again and again until it feels safe enough or okay enough. But even then, honestly, it still doesn’t always feel settled. I can end up worn out from it and still not feel sure I can leave it alone.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it’s not like “three times” or “five times,” more just until I feel like I can stop, if I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be perfect or flawless in any way?\n\nPatient: Perfect or flawless… I don’t know if I’d call it that, exactly. I’m not trying to make the house look perfect.\n\nIt’s more like I can’t leave things alone unless they feel okay enough. Even when I’m worn out, I still don’t always feel sure enough to stop.\n\nIs that what you mean by perfect?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be arranged in a way that looks or feels balanced to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not balanced like visually balanced, anyway. I’m not really arranging things to look a certain way.\n\nIt’s more that I notice something feels off and I try to make it feel okay, but I wouldn’t say I’m aiming for balance or a certain layout. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Even when I’m worn out, I still don’t always feel sure enough to stop.\n\nIs that what you mean by perfect?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be arranged in a way that looks or feels balanced to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not balanced like visually balanced, anyway. I’m not really arranging things to look a certain way.\n\nIt’s more that I notice something feels off and I try to make it feel okay, but I wouldn’t say I’m aiming for balance or a certain layout. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Exact or precise… maybe a little, but not like measuring something or doing it the “right” way exactly.

It’s more that I’ll keep checking the same outlets, locks, tools, or the dust until it feels okay enough to leave it. I know that probably sounds excessive. I don’t mean to make a big deal of it, but it can take up most of my day, and even after I’ve gone over it again and again, I’m worn out and still don’t always feel sure.

So… not exact in a technical way, I guess. More like I can’t get it to feel settled. Is that what you’re asking?

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42.0, F42.1, F42.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a hard time leaving things alone at home. I keep going back to check outlets, locks, tools, and even dust, and then I try to fix or straighten things again and again. It takes up most of my day, honestly, and by the end I’m worn out… but I still don’t really feel sure enough to stop. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts or images that keep repeating in your mind and are disturbing or upsetting to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but yes. Even when I’m pretty sure I did something right, I’ll get these thoughts that maybe I missed a loose wire and the house could burn down, or that I left a nail somewhere and my wife could get hurt. I know it probably sounds excessive… I try to push it out of my head, but then it comes back even stronger.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks or fixes in a specific way or order to feel okay?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know if it’s, like, a “proper” order or anything, but I do end up going back over the same things—outlets, locks, tools, even dust—and checking or fixing them again and again until it feels… not exactly okay, but closer.  \n\nI try to tell myself I’m just being careful, but it can take most of the day, and even when I’m worn out I still don’t feel sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checks and fixes are necessary to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s what it feels like. I know it might sound a bit over the top, and I don’t want to make it seem like I’m not reasonable, but if I don’t go back over the outlets, the locks, the tools, even the dust and little things around the house, I get this feeling like something could go wrong because I didn’t do it right.\n\nSo I keep checking and fixing, sometimes most of the day, until I’m worn out. And even then, I don’t really feel completely sure enough to leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking and fixing these things helps reduce your anxiety or worry about something bad happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… for a little while, I guess it does. Like if I go back over the outlets or the locks or the tools, or clean up the dust the “right” way, I feel a bit less worried for a moment.\n\nBut then it doesn’t really last. I end up checking and fixing the same things again and again, and it can take most of the day. By the end I’m worn out, and I still don’t feel completely sure enough to just leave it alone. I know that probably sounds like a lot… but that’s kind of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to prevent something specific from happening when you check and fix things, or is it more about feeling unsure and trying to feel more certain?\n\nPatient: I think it’s… both, maybe. I mean, there are specific things that pop into my head, and I don’t really like saying them because they sound awful, but… even when I know I did something right, I’ll suddenly think, what if I missed a loose wire and the house burns down? Or what if I left a nail somewhere and my wife gets hurt?\n\nI try to tell myself that’s not realistic, or push it away, but then it comes back even stronger. So I check and fix things to make that feeling go down and to feel more certain, I guess. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it… that’s just what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that looks or feels 'just right' or symmetrical?\n\nPatient: Um… not so much symmetrical, I don’t think. It’s more that it has to feel done enough, or done the right way, if that makes sense.\n\nLike with the outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house, I’ll keep going back over them and fixing little things again and again. I know it probably sounds excessive, but I can spend most of the day doing that, and even when I’m completely worn out, I still don’t feel quite sure enough to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a specific or perfect way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not perfect like I’m trying to make it look nice, exactly, but it has to feel like I did it the right way.\n\nI’ll go back over the same outlets, locks, tools, or dust in the house and check or fix them again and again. I don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but it can take up most of my day sometimes. And even when I’m worn out from it, I still don’t feel totally sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be arranged in a specific pattern or order?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a pattern or order, really. At least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nIt’s more like I’m trying to make sure it’s okay, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t have to arrange things in a certain sequence or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be a certain way or in a certain place to feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it does, but not really like everything has to be in some exact spot or lined up.\n\nIt’s more that outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house have to feel like they’re okay before I can leave them. I’ll check or fix the same things over and over, and I know that probably sounds like a lot… but I can lose most of the day to it. Even after I’m exhausted, it still doesn’t feel settled enough. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be symmetrical or arranged in a precise way to feel right?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think it’s symmetry exactly. I’m not trying to make things match or line them up perfectly, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I need it to feel okay before I can move on. But not in a precise arranged way, if that makes sense. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that feels exact or precise to you?\n\nPatient: Exact or precise… maybe a little, but not like measuring something or doing it the “right” way exactly.\n\nIt’s more that I’ll keep checking the same outlets, locks, tools, or the dust until it feels okay enough to leave it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so, but maybe not in a neat “specific steps” kind of way.

It’s more like I go back over the same things — the outlets, the locks, tools, dust around the house — and I’ll check or fix them again until it feels… right enough, I guess. I don’t like saying that because it sounds a bit ridiculous, but it ends up taking most of the day sometimes.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a hard time leaving things alone at home. I keep going back to check outlets, locks, tools, and even dust, and then I try to fix or straighten things again and again. It takes up most of my day, honestly, and by the end I’m worn out… but I still don’t really feel sure enough to stop. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts or images that keep repeating in your mind and are disturbing or upsetting to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but yes. Even when I’m pretty sure I did something right, I’ll get these thoughts that maybe I missed a loose wire and the house could burn down, or that I left a nail somewhere and my wife could get hurt. I know it probably sounds excessive… I try to push it out of my head, but then it comes back even stronger.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks or fixes in a specific way or order to feel okay?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know if it’s, like, a “proper” order or anything, but I do end up going back over the same things—outlets, locks, tools, even dust—and checking or fixing them again and again until it feels… not exactly okay, but closer.  \n\nI try to tell myself I’m just being careful, but it can take most of the day, and even when I’m worn out I still don’t feel sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checks and fixes are necessary to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s what it feels like. I know it might sound a bit over the top, and I don’t want to make it seem like I’m not reasonable, but if I don’t go back over the outlets, the locks, the tools, even the dust and little things around the house, I get this feeling like something could go wrong because I didn’t do it right.\n\nSo I keep checking and fixing, sometimes most of the day, until I’m worn out. And even then, I don’t really feel completely sure enough to leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking and fixing these things helps reduce your anxiety or worry about something bad happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… for a little while, I guess it does. Like if I go back over the outlets or the locks or the tools, or clean up the dust the “right” way, I feel a bit less worried for a moment.\n\nBut then it doesn’t really last. I end up checking and fixing the same things again and again, and it can take most of the day. By the end I’m worn out, and I still don’t feel completely sure enough to just leave it alone. I know that probably sounds like a lot… but that’s kind of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to prevent something specific from happening when you check and fix things, or is it more about feeling unsure and trying to feel more certain?\n\nPatient: I think it’s… both, maybe. I mean, there are specific things that pop into my head, and I don’t really like saying them because they sound awful, but… even when I know I did something right, I’ll suddenly think, what if I missed a loose wire and the house burns down? Or what if I left a nail somewhere and my wife gets hurt?\n\nI try to tell myself that’s not realistic, or push it away, but then it comes back even stronger. So I check and fix things to make that feeling go down and to feel more certain, I guess. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it… that’s just what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that looks or feels 'just right' or symmetrical?\n\nPatient: Um… not so much symmetrical, I don’t think. It’s more that it has to feel done enough, or done the right way, if that makes sense.\n\nLike with the outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house, I’ll keep going back over them and fixing little things again and again. I know it probably sounds excessive, but I can spend most of the day doing that, and even when I’m completely worn out, I still don’t feel quite sure enough to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a specific or perfect way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not perfect like I’m trying to make it look nice, exactly, but it has to feel like I did it the right way.\n\nI’ll go back over the same outlets, locks, tools, or dust in the house and check or fix them again and again. I don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but it can take up most of my day sometimes. And even when I’m worn out from it, I still don’t feel totally sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be arranged in a specific pattern or order?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a pattern or order, really. At least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nIt’s more like I’m trying to make sure it’s okay, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t have to arrange things in a certain sequence or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be a certain way or in a certain place to feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it does, but not really like everything has to be in some exact spot or lined up.\n\nIt’s more that outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house have to feel like they’re okay before I can leave them. I’ll check or fix the same things over and over, and I know that probably sounds like a lot… but I can lose most of the day to it. Even after I’m exhausted, it still doesn’t feel settled enough. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be symmetrical or arranged in a precise way to feel right?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think it’s symmetry exactly. I’m not trying to make things match or line them up perfectly, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I need it to feel okay before I can move on. But not in a precise arranged way, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be done a certain number of times to feel right?\n\nPatient: Not really a certain number, no… I don’t count it out like that.\n\nIt’s more that I go back over the same things — the outlets, locks, tools, dust — and I’ll check or fix them again and again until it feels safe enough or okay enough. I can end up worn out from it and still not feel sure I can leave it alone.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it’s not like “three times” or “five times,” more just until I feel like I can stop, if I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be perfect or flawless in any way?\n\nPatient: Perfect or flawless… I don’t know if I’d call it that, exactly. I’m not trying to make things match or line them up perfectly, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I need it to feel okay before I can move on. But not in a precise arranged way, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be done a certain number of times to feel right?\n\nPatient: Not really a certain number, no… I don’t count it out like that.\n\nIt’s more that I go back over the same things — the outlets, locks, tools, dust — and I’ll check or fix them again and again until it feels safe enough or okay enough. But even then, honestly, it still doesn’t always feel settled. I can end up worn out from it and still not feel sure I can leave it alone.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it’s not like “three times” or “five times,” more just until I feel like I can stop, if I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be perfect or flawless in any way?\n\nPatient: Perfect or flawless… I don’t know if I’d call it that, exactly. I’m not trying to make the house look perfect.\n\nIt’s more like I can’t leave things alone unless they feel okay enough. So I keep going back to the outlets, locks, tools, or dust, checking and fixing them again and again. I know it sounds like a lot, and I’m not proud of it, but it can take up most of the day. Even when I’m worn out, I still don’t always feel sure enough to stop.\n\nIs that what you mean by perfect?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be arranged in a way that looks or feels balanced to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not balanced like visually balanced, anyway. I’m not really arranging things to look a certain way.\n\nIt’s more that I notice something feels off and I try to make it feel okay, but I wouldn’t say I’m aiming for balance or a certain layout. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that feels exact or precise to you?\n\nPatient: Exact or precise… maybe a little, but not like measuring something or doing it the “right” way exactly.\n\nIt’s more that I’ll keep checking the same outlets, locks, tools, or the dust until it feels okay enough to leave it. I know that probably sounds excessive. I don’t mean to make a big deal of it, but it can take up most of my day, and even after I’ve gone over it again and again, I’m worn out and still don’t always feel sure.\n\nSo… not exact in a technical way, I guess. More like I can’t get it to feel settled. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be done in a very specific manner to feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, but maybe not in a neat “specific steps” kind of way.\n\nIt’s more like I go back over the same things — the outlets, the locks, tools, dust around the house — and I’ll check or fix them again until it feels… right enough, I guess. I don’t like saying that because it sounds a bit ridiculous, but it ends up taking most of the day sometimes.\n\nEven after I’ve done it over and over, I can be worn out and still not feel sure enough to just leave it alone. I mean, there are specific things that pop into my head, and I don’t really like saying them because they sound awful, but… even when I know I did something right, I’ll suddenly think, what if I missed a loose wire and the house burns down? Or what if I left a nail somewhere and my wife gets hurt?\n\nI try to tell myself that’s not realistic, or push it away, but then it comes back even stronger. So I check and fix things to make that feeling go down and to feel more certain, I guess. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it… that’s just what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that looks or feels 'just right' or symmetrical?\n\nPatient: Um… not so much symmetrical, I don’t think. It’s more that it has to feel done enough, or done the right way, if that makes sense.\n\nLike with the outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house, I’ll keep going back over them and fixing little things again and again. I know it probably sounds excessive, but I can spend most of the day doing that, and even when I’m completely worn out, I still don’t feel quite sure enough to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a specific or perfect way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not perfect like I’m trying to make it look nice, exactly, but it has to feel like I did it the right way.\n\nI’ll go back over the same outlets, locks, tools, or dust in the house and check or fix them again and again. I don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but it can take up most of my day sometimes. And even when I’m worn out from it, I still don’t feel totally sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be arranged in a specific pattern or order?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a pattern or order, really. At least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nIt’s more like I’m trying to make sure it’s okay, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t have to arrange things in a certain sequence or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be a certain way or in a certain place to feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it does, but not really like everything has to be in some exact spot or lined up.\n\nIt’s more that outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house have to feel like they’re okay before I can leave them. I’ll check or fix the same things over and over, and I know that probably sounds like a lot… but I can lose most of the day to it. Even after I’m exhausted, it still doesn’t feel settled enough. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be symmetrical or arranged in a precise way to feel right?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think it’s symmetry exactly. I’m not trying to make things match or line them up perfectly, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I need it to feel okay before I can move on. But not in a precise arranged way, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be done a certain number of times to feel right?\n\nPatient: Not really a certain number, no… I don’t count it out like that.\n\nIt’s more that I go back over the same things — the outlets, locks, tools, dust — and I’ll check or fix them again and again until it feels safe enough or okay enough. But even then, honestly, it still doesn’t always feel settled. I can end up worn out from it and still not feel sure I can leave it alone.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it’s not like “three times” or “five times,” more just until I feel like I can stop, if I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be perfect or flawless in any way?\n\nPatient: Perfect or flawless… I don’t know if I’d call it that, exactly. I’m not trying to make the house look perfect.\n\nIt’s more like I can’t leave things alone unless they feel okay enough. So I keep going back to the outlets, locks, tools, or dust, checking and fixing them again and again. I know it sounds like a lot, and I’m not proud of it, but it can take up most of the day. Even when I’m worn out, I still don’t always feel sure enough to stop.\n\nIs that what you mean by perfect?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be arranged in a way that looks or feels balanced to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not balanced like visually balanced, anyway. I’m not really arranging things to look a certain way.\n\nIt’s more that I notice something feels off and I try to make it feel okay, but I wouldn’t say I’m aiming for balance or a certain layout. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that feels exact or precise to you?\n\nPatient: Exact or precise… maybe a little, but not like measuring something or doing it the “right” way exactly.\n\nIt’s more that I’ll keep checking the same outlets, locks, tools, or the dust until it feels okay enough to leave it. I know that probably sounds excessive. I don’t mean to make a big deal of it, but it can take up most of my day, and even after I’ve gone over it again and again, I’m worn out and still don’t always feel sure.\n\nSo… not exact in a technical way, I guess. More like I can’t get it to feel settled. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be done in a very specific manner to feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, but maybe not in a neat “specific steps” kind of way.\n\nIt’s more like I go back over the same things — the outlets, the locks, tools, dust around the house — and I’ll check or fix them again until it feels… right enough, I guess. I don’t like saying that because it sounds a bit ridiculous, but it ends up taking most of the day sometimes.\n\nEven after I’ve done it over and over, I can be worn out and still not feel sure enough to just leave it alone. Is that the kind of “specific manner” you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that feels complete or finished to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… “complete” is probably a good word for it.\n\nI’ll go back over the outlets, the locks, tools, dust around the house, and I keep checking or fixing them until it feels finished enough to stop. But the frustrating part is it doesn’t really stay feeling finished. I can spend most of the day doing it, and by the end I’m worn out, but I still don’t always feel sure enough to just leave it alone.\n\nI know that sounds like a lot… I’m trying to explain it clearly. Is that what you mean by complete?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I think it’s more about preventing mistakes… or at least that’s what it feels like to me.

I’m here because I spend most of the day going back over the same things — outlets, locks, tools, even dust around the house — checking and fixing them again and again. I keep trying to get to a point where I feel sure enough to leave it alone, but I usually end up worn out and still not really feeling sure.

I don’t know if that sounds like needing control too, maybe it does… but in my head it’s more like, “What if I missed something?” Does that make sense?

Even when I’m pretty sure I did something right, I’ll get these thoughts that maybe I missed a loose wire and the house could burn down, or that I left a nail somewhere and my wife could get hurt. I know it probably sounds excessive… I try to push it out of my head, but then it comes back even stronger.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checks or fixes in a specific way or order to feel okay?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know if it’s, like, a “proper” order or anything, but I do end up going back over the same things—outlets, locks, tools, even dust—and checking or fixing them again and again until it feels… not exactly okay, but closer.  \n\nI try to tell myself I’m just being careful, but it can take most of the day, and even when I’m worn out I still don’t feel sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checks and fixes are necessary to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s what it feels like. I know it might sound a bit over the top, and I don’t want to make it seem like I’m not reasonable, but if I don’t go back over the outlets, the locks, the tools, even the dust and little things around the house, I get this feeling like something could go wrong because I didn’t do it right.\n\nSo I keep checking and fixing, sometimes most of the day, until I’m worn out. And even then, I don’t really feel completely sure enough to leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking and fixing these things helps reduce your anxiety or worry about something bad happening?\n\nPatient: Yeah… for a little while, I guess it does. Like if I go back over the outlets or the locks or the tools, or clean up the dust the “right” way, I feel a bit less worried for a moment.\n\nBut then it doesn’t really last. I end up checking and fixing the same things again and again, and it can take most of the day. By the end I’m worn out, and I still don’t feel completely sure enough to just leave it alone. I know that probably sounds like a lot… but that’s kind of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to prevent something specific from happening when you check and fix things, or is it more about feeling unsure and trying to feel more certain?\n\nPatient: I think it’s… both, maybe. I mean, there are specific things that pop into my head, and I don’t really like saying them because they sound awful, but… even when I know I did something right, I’ll suddenly think, what if I missed a loose wire and the house burns down? Or what if I left a nail somewhere and my wife gets hurt?\n\nI try to tell myself that’s not realistic, or push it away, but then it comes back even stronger. So I check and fix things to make that feeling go down and to feel more certain, I guess. I’m not trying to be dramatic about it… that’s just what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that looks or feels 'just right' or symmetrical?\n\nPatient: Um… not so much symmetrical, I don’t think. It’s more that it has to feel done enough, or done the right way, if that makes sense.\n\nLike with the outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house, I’ll keep going back over them and fixing little things again and again. I know it probably sounds excessive, but I can spend most of the day doing that, and even when I’m completely worn out, I still don’t feel quite sure enough to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a specific or perfect way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not perfect like I’m trying to make it look nice, exactly, but it has to feel like I did it the right way.\n\nI’ll go back over the same outlets, locks, tools, or dust in the house and check or fix them again and again. I don’t mean to make it sound worse than it is, but it can take up most of my day sometimes. And even when I’m worn out from it, I still don’t feel totally sure enough to just leave it alone. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be arranged in a specific pattern or order?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a pattern or order, really. At least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nIt’s more like I’m trying to make sure it’s okay, if that makes sense. But no, I don’t have to arrange things in a certain sequence or anything like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be a certain way or in a certain place to feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it does, but not really like everything has to be in some exact spot or lined up.\n\nIt’s more that outlets, locks, tools, or even dust around the house have to feel like they’re okay before I can leave them. I’ll check or fix the same things over and over, and I know that probably sounds like a lot… but I can lose most of the day to it. Even after I’m exhausted, it still doesn’t feel settled enough. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be symmetrical or arranged in a precise way to feel right?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think it’s symmetry exactly. I’m not trying to make things match or line them up perfectly, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nIt’s more just… I need it to feel okay before I can move on. But not in a precise arranged way, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be done a certain number of times to feel right?\n\nPatient: Not really a certain number, no… I don’t count it out like that.\n\nIt’s more that I go back over the same things — the outlets, locks, tools, dust — and I’ll check or fix them again and again until it feels safe enough or okay enough. But even then, honestly, it still doesn’t always feel settled. I can end up worn out from it and still not feel sure I can leave it alone.\n\nI hope that makes sense… it’s not like “three times” or “five times,” more just until I feel like I can stop, if I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be perfect or flawless in any way?\n\nPatient: Perfect or flawless… I don’t know if I’d call it that, exactly. I’m not trying to make the house look perfect.\n\nIt’s more like I can’t leave things alone unless they feel okay enough. So I keep going back to the outlets, locks, tools, or dust, checking and fixing them again and again. I know it sounds like a lot, and I’m not proud of it, but it can take up most of the day. Even when I’m worn out, I still don’t always feel sure enough to stop.\n\nIs that what you mean by perfect?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be arranged in a way that looks or feels balanced to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not balanced like visually balanced, anyway. I’m not really arranging things to look a certain way.\n\nIt’s more that I notice something feels off and I try to make it feel okay, but I wouldn’t say I’m aiming for balance or a certain layout. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that feels exact or precise to you?\n\nPatient: Exact or precise… maybe a little, but not like measuring something or doing it the “right” way exactly.\n\nIt’s more that I’ll keep checking the same outlets, locks, tools, or the dust until it feels okay enough to leave it. I know that probably sounds excessive. I don’t mean to make a big deal of it, but it can take up most of my day, and even after I’ve gone over it again and again, I’m worn out and still don’t always feel sure.\n\nSo… not exact in a technical way, I guess. More like I can’t get it to feel settled. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix have to be done in a very specific manner to feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, but maybe not in a neat “specific steps” kind of way.\n\nIt’s more like I go back over the same things — the outlets, the locks, tools, dust around the house — and I’ll check or fix them again until it feels… right enough, I guess. I don’t like saying that because it sounds a bit ridiculous, but it ends up taking most of the day sometimes.\n\nEven after I’ve done it over and over, I can be worn out and still not feel sure enough to just leave it alone. Is that the kind of “specific manner” you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the things you check and fix need to be done in a way that feels complete or finished to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… “complete” is probably a good word for it.\n\nI’ll go back over the outlets, the locks, tools, dust around the house, and I keep checking or fixing them until it feels finished enough to stop. But the frustrating part is it doesn’t really stay feeling finished. I can spend most of the day doing it, and by the end I’m worn out, but I still don’t always feel sure enough to just leave it alone.\n\nI know that sounds like a lot… I’m trying to explain it clearly. Is that what you mean by complete?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your checking and fixing is ever driven by a need to make things symmetrical or perfectly arranged?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really symmetrical, like lining things up evenly or making both sides match.\n\nI mean, I might straighten something if it looks obviously off, but that doesn’t feel like the main thing driving it. It’s more… I’m not sure how to put it. I haven’t noticed that it’s about perfect arranging, at least not in the way you’re asking.\n\nDoes that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your checking behavior is more about preventing something bad from happening because of a mistake you might have made?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s closer to it.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I know a lot of the time I did the job right. But these thoughts jump in, like maybe I missed a loose wire and it could burn the house down, or I left a nail somewhere and my wife could get hurt.\n\nI try to tell myself it’s fine and push it away, but then it comes back stronger, and that’s when I end up checking again. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to check and fix things is more about being in control of your environment or preventing mistakes from happening?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more about preventing mistakes… or at least that’s what it feels like to me.\n\nI’m here because I spend most of the day going back over the same things — outlets, locks, tools, even dust around the house — checking and fixing them again and again. I keep trying to get to a point where I feel sure enough to leave it alone, but I usually end up worn out and still not really feeling sure.\n\nI don’t know if that sounds like needing control too, maybe it does… but in my head it’s more like, “What if I missed something?” Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
